> **⚙️ STANDING CONVENTION — D-121: THREE NAMED LENSES (Joe, locked 2026-07-19; THIRD LENS ADDED 2026-07-26).** ANNOTATION AT THE SITE (D-131, J-718, 2026-08-12): THIS SIGNPOST READ *TWO NAMED LENSES* AND WAS THREE WEEKS STALE. DECISIONS.md:4504 IS THE RULE AND READS **THREE**: (1) user-visible impact, (2) **TIER CONSEQUENCE**, (3) resource cost - amended 2026-07-26 on J-591's evidence (Joe: *assertion against t4 is fundamental ... among user view and resource drain*). **LENS 2's FOUR QUESTIONS** (DECISIONS.md:4518): does crypto-shred remain a real guarantee (D-093) - does a T4 durability floor survive, since a floor with one copy is not a floor - whose tier governs, and is that decided DELIBERATELY or BY ACCIDENT (if the answer is whoever acted first, that is a race, not a decision) - is one party's erasure-fate silently imposed on another. **NO TIER CONSEQUENCE IS A LEGAL AND EXPECTED ANSWER** - most tooling, probes, records and widget-layout questions have none; say so plainly, and never manufacture a tier rationale, which is as bad as a manufactured UX one. **IT RANKS ABOVE RESOURCE COST BECAUSE A TIER BREACH IS A CONSTRAINT VIOLATION, NOT AN EXPENSE** - it cannot be bought off with build time, and it is often invisible until the moment it matters. **THE COST OF THIS STALENESS, MEASURED: the J-718 kickoff said two lenses, and the Leg E Phase-0 as first written said two** - N-109's shape at CONVENTION scale, in the one document whose entire job is to be read first, found by Joe's recall and not by any re-read. **READ DECISIONS.md D-121 AS THE RULE; THIS LINE IS A SIGNPOST.** Everything below about lenses 1 and 3 stands verbatim. **THE ORIGINAL TEXT FOLLOWS.** Before any option list reaches Joe and before any recommendation is made, state **① user-visible impact PER OPTION** — what does a person using XGen see, feel, or come to believe? — then **② resource cost** (build time, bundle size, maintenance and test surface, future work created). Architectural elegance, symmetry and implementation convenience are **tertiary**: still recorded, never leading. **⚠️ ADDITIONAL LENSES, NOT A REPLACEMENT** — D-065, D6, N-091, D-110/D-111, D-067, the no-anonymity core and GDPR discipline stand **undiminished**; D-121 adds a pass, it does not mint a trump card. **⚠️ A COLLISION GOES TO JOE UNRESOLVED, NAMED AS A COLLISION** — never silently traded, above all where identity, the wire, or anything irreversible is touched: *a rule that can be outranked by an appeal to user experience is not a rule, and the trades this project exists to refuse are exactly the ones that feel good to a user in the moment.* **⚠️ "NO USER-FACING IMPACT" IS A LEGAL AND EXPECTED ANSWER** — say it plainly and decide on cost; *a manufactured UX rationale is worse than none, because it launders an internal preference as a user's interest.* **THE REASON GOVERNS THE APPLICATION:** Joe locks architecture but cannot independently verify most technical claims put to him — these two axes are the ones he CAN judge, so stating them is what makes a recommendation **checkable rather than trusted blind**. **🔑 THE EVIDENCE (J-559):** Chat recommended the D2 composer read the selection bus on a sound architectural argument; **Joe asked what it would do to the user's experience**, and reading `stream-panel.svelte` showed **R5 latches the room** — so the composer would have **greyed out while the user was still looking at the conversation**, on ordinary navigation, unexplained. **The recommendation flipped.** *The architecture argument was not wrong; it answered a question that ranks below the one that decides.* **PHASE-0 CONSEQUENCE:** the user-visible consequence must be **GROUNDED, not reasoned** — read the code, run the probe, say what the user sees; *a principle that says "decide by user impact" without a step that MEASURES it merely relocates the guessing.* **CLAUDE'S HONEST BOUNDARY:** resource cost is **measurable** and must be measured rather than estimated; **user experience is only ever INFERRED** — Claude never observes a user, so on many such calls **Joe is the better judge**, which is exactly why they are stated for him instead of acted on silently.

> **⚙️ STANDING CONVENTION — DESIGNATION COLLISIONS AND ADDENDA: SEE `DECISIONS.md` → D-134 (Joe, confirmed + amended 2026-07-29, J-619). THAT ENTRY IS THE RULE; THIS IS A SIGNPOST.** Applies to `J-` · `D-` · `N-` · `M-` alike. 🔒 **Designations are ISSUED UNIQUE** — the lettered split is a **repair applied at revision**, never an issuing path. **① COLLISION SPLIT:** one designation issued more than once for unrelated things → copies take `a`, `b`, `c`… **in the order they appear IN THE RECORD**, and the bare number is **RETIRED** (7 of 7 in `DECISIONS.md`: `D-030 · 031 · 037 · 038 · 039 · 055 · 056`). **② ADDENDUM:** a follow-on that extends or corrects an existing record takes the next letter and the bare number **SURVIVES** (`N-124` + `a` + `b`; `M-RP2.30` + `a`; `M-RP2.31` + `a`). 🔑 **DISCRIMINATOR — ask whether the bare number still exists.** Present ⇒ addenda. Absent ⇒ collision split. **No third notation is needed, which is why retiring the bare number is NORMATIVE.** 🛑 **`a` = FIRST IN THE RECORD, NOT FIRST IN THE FILE** — in a newest-first file (`JOURNAL_ARCHIVE.md`) the **higher line number is the earlier record**. 🛑 **NEVER renumber one side to a free number** — every existing citation would silently point at the wrong record; every citation of a retired bare number is re-pointed **individually**.

> **⚙️ STANDING CONVENTION — NO BACKTICKS IN ANY POWERSHELL GIVEN TO JOE (locked 2026-07-17).** Joe hates backtick (`` ` ``) line-continuation — a stray trailing space silently breaks it, and it is not a real character for this purpose. When Claude (Chat **or** Clair) prepares a commit/push or any PS block for Joe: put each command (each `git …`) on **ONE physical line** — multiple `-m` flags on the same line — **or** use a here-string (`@" … "@`, opener at line-end, closing `"@` at column 0) for long multi-paragraph commit bodies. **No backtick line-continuation, ever.** (Git workflow otherwise unchanged: explicit `git add <file>` per file, never `git add .`; `git status` before commit; Joe pushes.)

> **🎨 APPEARANCE MODEL — the CSS layer stack is CANONICAL (J-504/J-505, `ui/docs/xgen-css-layer-model.md` v1.1). READ IT BEFORE TOUCHING ANY GLYPH OR SKIN RULE.** The *appearance* sibling of `xgen-region-dock-model.md` (which owns *layout*). **The stack, Joe-locked:** `theme-*.css` (custom skin, the override layer) → **`skin.css` + `glyphs.generated.css` — ONE layer, the default skin, split by WHO WRITES IT** (hand vs generated) → the normalizes (**reset, not skin**). **A theme overrides a glyph exactly the way it overrides a colour — the cascade IS the mechanism.** **D-108: a glyph is a SKIN TOKEN — `core` owns the NAME (content), the skin owns the SHAPE (geometry).** A component never writes geometry, for the same reason it never writes a colour (N-025/N-090). **CDP-proven:** CSS **`d:`** works in WebView2 · **`var()` resolves inside a custom-property value** → **ONE** skin rule (`.icon path { d: var(--g) }`) serves every icon · a later stylesheet **redraws any glyph with zero component change**. **D-109: the Chromium/WebView2 `d:` dependency is DELIBERATE and NOT hedged** — the WebKit exit is the `-url` form the bank already emits.

> **🔒 D-110 — THE SPACE-THEME BAN. A TRUST BOUNDARY, NOT A STYLE RULE. Ch6 §6.2 REWRITTEN + §6.3.1/§6.3.2 NEW (Ch6 v0.5).** **A SPACE MAY RE-COLOUR. A SPACE MAY NOT RE-DRAW, AND MAY NOT RE-LAYOUT.** Ch6 §6.3's Layer 3 is a theme **declared by a Space OWNER and delivered over the wire** — unrestricted, it would let a Space owner **redraw a lock, a warning, a verified mark, or the AI badge (§6.13)**: icon spoofing in an identity-verified protocol. Colour (incl. the glyph **tint**) ✅ · **geometry (`--glyph-*`, `--glyph-*-url`) ❌** · layout/metrics ❌ · **everything not on the allowlist ❌ by default** (allowlist, never denylist). **🔑 TWO NORMATIVE CONSEQUENCES, both binding on future code:** **(a) A KEY ALLOWLIST ALONE IS THEATRE** — a Space theme is a key→value **map**, but a client that **string-concatenates a stylesheet** lets a malicious **VALUE** escape its declaration and inject arbitrary CSS. **MANDATORY: apply via `element.style.setProperty()` (CSSOM cannot break out) + validate with `CSS.supports()` + scope to the Space subtree — ALL THREE; any one alone is insufficient.** **NEVER interpolate a wire value into a `<style>` text node.** **(b) THE BAN BINDS THE GENERATOR BACK** — **`--glyph-*-url` MUST be emitted COLOUR-FREE** (a `currentColor` mask), because a data-URI with colour baked in **fuses colour and geometry into ONE token** → the seven `%23e6e6e6` glyph re-emits are now a **SECURITY REQUIREMENT**, not a tidy-up. *(Lesson worth keeping: **an access rule is only real if the data model can express the distinction it draws.**)* **Locked BEFORE implementation — `state.space_theme` is in NO Rust, NO TS, NO Svelte. `theme-*.css` DOES NOT EXIST. No milestone may claim theming works, and none may ship a Layer-3 applier that does not implement Ch6 §6.3.2 in full.** **Ch6 §6.2's four-layer model (D-057/D-058) is superseded IN PART** — intent survives (minimal reset, 13px/1.35, 4px unit); **`tokens.css` never existed, and component `<style>` blocks are FORBIDDEN, not required** — *the rule that makes skinning TOTAL is the rule that forbids the component from participating in it.* **Still open:** the exact colour-token allowlist · user-disable of Space themes.  

> **🔒 D-111 — A CLIENT MUST NOT FETCH A HOST CHOSEN BY SOMEONE ELSE (J-506, Ch2 v1.3, N-103).** **⚠️ OPENS WITH A RETRACTION — J-504/J-505 listed *"`url()` fetches"* as an open trust surface. IT IS NOT ONE.** Under **D-110's own** colour-only allowlist, `CSS.supports('color','url(…)')` is **false** — a `url()` **cannot enter a Space theme**. Fonts are **bundled** (Ch6 §6.2). *A threat named from pattern-matching, in the same doc that forecloses it — the SECOND overclaim in two turns on one topic. Retracted, not hedged.* **🔑 THE REAL INVARIANT, and the protocol had ALREADY WON IT:** *a client that fetches a URL chosen by **someone else** is a **BEACON** — it leaks the reader's **IP** and **read-time** to a host of the **sender's** choosing. **XGen publishes your XGID by design; it does NOT publish your network location.*** **Every avenue is already shut, structurally:** `message.image`/`message.file` carry **`xgen://hash/sha256:<64-hex>` — a CONTENT ADDRESS, NOT A LOCATION** (`xgen-core/src/blob_store.rs`; federation-native M12; per-blob client-encrypted; store *"content-blind by construction"*) · **no HTTP client in any crate** · fonts bundled · themes URL-free. **🔑 A HASH CANNOT NAME A HOST — the beacon is not *blocked*, it is UNSAYABLE.** *Content-addressing was taken for integrity and bought privacy for free; nobody had written it down.* **⚠️ THE ONE ROW THAT INVITES IT BACK: `link previews`** (Ch2's *"Client decisions — implementation freedom"* table). Unbuilt — but a client fetching preview URLs from message text hands the **sender** every reader's IP + read-time, invisibly, on every message. **✅ RULE: link previews — and ANY rendering that resolves an outbound URL — are fetched NODE-SIDE, NEVER client-side.** The Node already talks to the world; the Client deliberately does not. One fetch per link, not one per reader. *Freedom preserved: **whether** and **how** to preview stay the client's; only the fetch **LOCATION** is fixed — because it is not a rendering decision, it is **a privacy boundary wearing a rendering decision's clothes**.* **⚠️ WHAT ACTUALLY REMAINS, AND IT DWARFS EVERY GLYPH AND TOKEN: D-036 MODULE WIDGETS** — third-party HTML in an isolated webview; **CSP/sandboxing still OPEN at Ch6 §6.8.8** (filed Session 2, untouched since). **Unlike blobs, themes and glyphs, it has NO structural foreclosure at all.** Ch6's, not the UI track's. **Filed, not solved.** *Standing lesson: **a threat list padded with a foreclosed item is worse than a short one** — it trains the reader to skim, and the real entry gets skimmed with it. **Retract, don't hedge.***  

> **✅ D-112 + D-113 — THE PLUGIN TAXONOMY CLOSED AND THE MODULE-UI SANDBOX FLOOR LOCKED (J-507, Ch6 v0.6, `docs/xgen-plugin-taxonomy-phase0.md`).** **Locked together — you cannot classify a thing while leaving open what it is allowed to do.** **🔑 THE FRAME WAS ALREADY IN THE CODE:** `xgen-common/src/module.rs` (SE-D2) already says *"one unified handshake mechanism; the code term `kind` carries the system/ui distinction — a **module** is a *system* plugin (`host = node`), a **plugin** is a *ui* plugin (`host = client`)"*, plus **UUIDv4 slot/impl GUIDs that never federate** and *"the descriptor is a **const in the plugin's own code** — there is **no manifest file**; metadata is authoritative, location is never trusted."* *(Honest limit: the vocabulary is shipped; the `host`/`kind` FIELDS are not — `Descriptor` is `{kind_id, impl_id, name, assurance}`.)* **D-112 — ONE plugin, THREE axes:** **`host`** (node = system · client = ui) · **`delivery`** (`compiled` · `service` · `packaged`) · **`surface`** (`none`·`region`·`shelf`·`window`, at most one). ***"Module" and "widget" are not two species.*** **The drift was never Ch6-vs-D-102 — it was §6.8 vs EVERYTHING THAT WAS BUILT** (§6.8 predates the spine, the Auth registry, the widget tier, the region model and `WidgetMount`; **§6.8 moved**). **🔑 THE SLOT INVENTORY IS NOT A RIVAL — WE ALREADY BUILT IT:** half regions, half **content anchors inside a host widget**, and `message.details: WidgetMount[]` + unknown-id drop (W-13) **is** `room.message.decorator` **under another name** → **ONE placement model + ONE containment model. A slot is declared by the HOST, never requested by the guest.** **A THIRD species neither spec listed:** the **Auth Module is `delivery: service`** (`AuthModuleXgid` + `endpoint_url` + revocation — Ch6 §6.8.7 **corrected**). **Settings = `surface: window`; NO `screen` kind** (Discord full-overlay foreclosed knowingly — reversible on one word). **🔒 D-113 — THE BOUNDARY IS *DELIVERY*, NOT "WIDGET":** `self-panel` needs no CSP; **being `packaged` is what is dangerous** — the question sat on the wrong noun for three months. **S-1: a packaged module UI is a webview with NO NETWORK** (only its own local IPC channel) → **D-111's beacon is UNSAYABLE inside a module** · S-2 packaged assets · S-3 own origin, **no Tauri IPC** · **S-4 the module NEVER holds the key** (consent ≠ key handover; **our Rust signs, per event**) · **S-5 no trust chrome** (never the identity/verified/**AI-badge** zone — D-110 generalised) · S-6 deny-by-default caps · **S-7 no `packaged` plugin loads until S-1…S-6 ship** (**zero lines exist today → the lock costs nothing and forecloses everything**). **CLOSES Ch6 §6.8.8's widget-sandboxing question (open since April 2026)** + *module signing* + *module permissions*. **⚠️ NAMED, NOT SOLVED: the `compiled` LOADER is still open (`temperature.rs`) — a DYNAMIC LIBRARY IS NOT A SANDBOX; > **🔒 M-RP6.1k — DESIGN LOCKED 2026-07-12 (J-510 → D-114 + D-115). Runbook `tasks/M_RP6_1K_UISTATE_STORE.md` handed to Clair. 🟢 PLAY.** **🔑 TWO SPECS DISAGREED ABOUT WHO PERSISTS THE LAYOUT, AND GROUNDING — NOT RE-DERIVATION — SETTLED IT.** region-dock §9 specified a **layout-only** store (`xgen-client_layout.json` → `xgen-client_layouts.json`, five verbs, auto at **M-RP7.3** / named at **M-RP7.6**); surfaces §4 specified a **UI-state** store. **They were never two objects — §9 is simply the EARLIER, NARROWER DRAFT**, written before window geometry, the shelf, or a named-workspace concept existed. **🔒 D-114: ONE store — `xgen-client_uistate.json`.** §9's identity/reconcile rules **survive verbatim** as the `layout` key's own (`widgetId` durable · drop unknown · re-inject `system` · `version`+migrate); its **two filenames, its five verbs, and its “the layout manager is itself a widget” line are SUPERSEDED** (region-dock **v1.8**). Auto-save/load **and** named states are **both pulled forward from 7.3/7.6 into 6.1k** — S-8 already locked manual **named** states as *permanent, not a stopgap*, and the shelf faces dispatch them. **🔑 THE CARVE-OUT — AND IT IS THE REAL CONTENT OF THE LOCK: §4's inherited *“Rust persists an OPAQUE blob, never learns the shape”* rule CANNOT hold uniformly, and §4.2 is what breaks it.** The monitor-work-area **clamp is MANDATORY**, and **only Rust can read a work area, or apply a rect before the webview exists** (apply it later and the window visibly jumps). ***A clamp Rust cannot perform is not a clamp.*** → **`geometry` = a TYPED Rust struct** (`x,y,w,h,maximized`) **· every other key = an opaque `serde_json::Value`, round-tripped verbatim.** **This is J-499's rule APPLIED, not excepted:** J-499 killed `get_layout` because Rust would have **duplicated a type the webview owns** — **geometry is the OPPOSITE case**, a type **Rust already owns and the webview cannot**. ***Rust owns what only Rust can do, and stays blind to what the webview owns.*** *(A rule stated once and then applied without asking which side of it each thing falls on is how a rule becomes a superstition.)* Free consequence: an opaque value **preserves unknown keys** → every future key is additive with **zero Rust change**. **⚠️ AND THE FINDING THAT SHAPED THE MILESTONE — GREPPED, NOT ASSUMED: FIVE OF THE SIX §4.5 KEYS HAVE NO LIVE SOURCE TODAY.** Only **window geometry** has a feeder (resize grip + native decorations — the user moves it every session). **grid layout** = a **const** (`DEFAULT_LAYOUT`; no user mutation until renderer B) · **shelf favourites** = waiting on ④ pinning (the top strip mounts empty) · **collapsed/expanded** = **ZERO `collapsible` props anywhere in `ui/client`** (the 8 leaves are non-collapsible `section`s) · **theme** = `theme-*.css` **DOES NOT EXIST** (D-110) · **last open room** = no room selection until **M-RP6.2**. → **6.1k SHIPS `geometry` + `layout` ONLY, AND RESERVES NOTHING.** An empty `theme: null` key is the ***`tabs`-branch / N-091 shape at file scale*** — **a key nothing writes is a key nobody has round-tripped**; each remaining key lands **with the milestone that creates its source**. *(§4.5 is honoured, not narrowed: §4.5 locked what BELONGS in a UI state; **D-065 governs WHEN each one is built**.)* **🔒 D-115 — M-RP-WINSTATE's own written criterion FIRES → B** (own it; **`tauri-plugin-window-state` NOT taken**; M-RP-WINSTATE → **⬛ SUPERSEDED, absorbed**). **Unit = PHYSICAL px** (N-092b, measured twice) — ***a rect can only be compared to a monitor work area in the same unit, so a logical rect makes the mandatory clamp unimplementable***. **Clamp-don't-refuse EXERCISED** (write an off-screen rect, launch, watch it centre). **⚠️ AND N-095's DoD MOVES FROM M-RP7.3 TO 6.1k:** its deferral's premise was that `loadLayout()` **cannot return null** — and **6.1k is the milestone that kills that premise** (it is where `loadLayout()` stops returning a constant and starts parsing a real file). Leaving it at 7.3 would mean **7.3 closing a hole 6.1k opened**. ***A deferral is valid only as long as its premise holds; when the premise dies, the deferral dies with it — it does not quietly inherit a new one.*** **RENAMED before the commands ever existed (2 lines): `layout.save`/`layout.load` → `uistate.save`/`uistate.load`** — **the store is NOT a layout** (it holds geometry, and will hold shelf/theme/room); `layout.*` would be a lie by 6.2. **No `saveAs` id** — one diskette, one dialog, two outcomes. **LEGS — VISIBLE FIRST (Joe's standing brief: see the UI early, correct it while the milestone is open):** **A** save + load **dialogs** (shell-local, the `about-dialog` precedent) + **both commands enter `commandTable`** + **`diskette`/`load` FLIP ENABLED**, against an in-memory store → *the milestone's UI is on screen and correctable before one line of plumbing exists* · **B** Rust `get_ui_state`/`set_ui_state` + the file + the `loadLayout()` body swap + **N-095 EXERCISED** · **C** geometry save (`CloseRequested` + debounced move/resize) + restore + **clamp EXERCISED** · **D** named states carry geometry (§4.2) + session key + CDP verify → close. **Real client 9222 ONLY** — no sampler cells; **the sampler catalogue must stay 328, and that is a verify leg**. **`cargo test` MUST MOVE from 1507/0/62** — the honest signal that Rust landed (the inverse of the 6.1i/6.1j *“identical to baseline PROVES no-Rust”* leg). **Client registry baseline: 46, read QUIESCENT (N-105).**  

> **✅ M-RP6.1k CLOSED (J-511) — THE UI-STATE STORE SHIPS. Then Legs B/C/D shipped persistence and nobody swept it** — so the milestone reached close-out with a store that survives a relaunch **while the app told the user their workspace was not being saved.** Caught in the **painted DOM** at Rule-5 re-drive, with two named states sitting on disk to prove the contradiction rather than argue it. ***A STALE HONESTY NOTE IS STILL A FALSE STATEMENT — and it is WORSE than a missing one, because it was written by someone being careful, so the next reader trusts it.*** **The discipline already existed and was not generalised:** 6.1j bound *“the disabled face is a COUNTDOWN, not a resting state”* — **a W-8 disclosure is the same object.** The face got swept **because the DoD named it**; the note got missed **because nothing named it**. → **RULE: no milestone closes leaving its own UI asserting a phase-limit it has since removed. Cost of the fix: **4 lines**. It was the cheapest defect in the milestone and **the only one that would have reached a human**.)* **🔒 D-114 realised LITERALLY:** `get_ui_state -> String` / `set_ui_state(json: String)` — a **raw-string opaque round-trip**; **ZERO descriptor types in the Rust crate** (the only `widgetId` in `xgen-client` is a raw string *inside a test*, which is the **proof of opacity, not a breach**); `WindowGeometry {x,y,width,height,maximized}` is the **only** typed part. ***Rust owns what only Rust can do, and stays blind to what the webview owns.*** **🔒 D-115 realised:** physical px throughout; clamp-don't-refuse **exercised** (off-screen rect → centred); **N-095 relocated here and exercised** (corrupt store → `DEFAULT_LAYOUT`, region-shell present, **no blank centre** — the J-499 30→21 failure **proven absent**); **`tauri-plugin-window-state` NOT taken**. **RESERVE NOTHING held — and it is visible on disk:** top-level keys are **exactly** `version`/`session`/`named`/`active`. No `theme`, no `shelf`, no `collapsed`, no `room`. **D-101 untouched** (config only) — the store is the project's **first deliberately persistent user-facing state**, by design. **🔑 N-108 — THE REGISTRY BREATHES WITH *STORE CONTENTS*, NOT JUST MENU STATE (extends N-105):** the Load dialog's picker/Load/Delete sit inside `{#if entries.length}` → quiescent count is **55 empty-store** and **59 with a saved state**. ***A baseline that depends on a DATA FILE is a baseline that can be wrong on a machine where nothing is wrong*** — **assert what is IN THE STORE, not merely that the popups are shut.** *(J-509's phantom one layer down.)* **Measured as a TRANSITION in ONE session — 55 → save → 59 → delete → 55**, exact return, zero leaks; a number **derived** by arithmetic was **refused entry to the record until it had been seen**. **🔑 N-107 — TWO WRITERS, ONE FILE:** Rust merges `session.geometry`, the frontend merges `named`/`active` — **read-modify-write on BOTH sides**, neither serialising a whole-file model it does not own. ***Any format with more than one writer must be MERGED, never REPLACED*** — whole-file serialisation by a narrow writer is **data loss wearing a save button's clothes**. Proven on disk: emptying `named` left `session.geometry` **byte-intact**. **MEASURED (Chat re-drove every leg, Rule 5 — not one number taken on report):** client registry **55** quiescent empty-store (`count===unique`) → **59** with a state · **`cargo test` 1517/0/62** (from 1507, **+10**, summed programmatically not hand-counted) · `vite build` **165** · `npm test` **41** · sampler catalogue **328 UNCHANGED** (by scope: zero `ui/core`/`ui/sampler` across both commits) · `:modal` true (on `:modal`, never the `open` attribute) · split ratios `[1,2,7,2]` **exact at a THIRD distinct width** · unknown key **survived the app's own write-back** · zero component-local `<style>`, accent-neutral. **Two legs in NOBODY's matrix, run at verify:** (1) **loading a named state with NO `geometry` key** — the disk had *grown* one (`001`, saved before Leg C existed; **real data outran the fixtures inside one session**) → window **unmoved**, centre alive, **both guards held** (`if (s?.layout)` / `if (s?.geometry)` — the J-499 blank-centre trap **designed around and cited by name** before it was ever hit); (2) **Delete is TWO-STEP** — the button re-labels to **“Confirm delete”** (**S-6's *destruction is deliberate* honoured inside the dialog**, and never in the DoD). **And “static gates need the apps down” stopped being folklore:** `cargo test` with the client up dies in 15 s on `failed to remove file …xgen-client.exe` — **the running app holds the binary**. **Deviations (Rule 6, flagged not absorbed):** shell-local `.svelte.ts` store (**the better call** — the exact seam Leg B swapped into) · comboboxes on both dialogs (Joe, mid-flight) · autofocus focus-catcher (so `showModal()` doesn't pop the combobox) · **the core `dialog` footer suppressed via `:has()`** → its `__close` stays **mounted-but-hidden and still registers** → **FILED, NOT BUILT: a footer snippet slot on the `dialog` core** (**correctly kept out** — a `core` change inside a shell milestone is what makes a registry delta unreadable; its own milestone) · **`quit`-command geometry save — a genuine find made IN VERIFICATION:** `app.exit` (Ctrl+Q / File→Exit) does **not** reliably fire `CloseRequested`, so the final rect would have been **silently lost on that path**. *Found because someone drove the app, not because someone read the code.* **+N-107/N-108/N-109; > **✅ M-RP6.1l CLOSED (J-513) — THE PLUGIN LIST SHIPS. Commit `1dc5849` (6 files, +272/−17). 🟢 NEXT-ACTIVE = M-RP6.2 — R1 Spaces + R2 Rooms on real `KnownSpace`. BACK TO THE GRID.** **The gear is enabled; NO shelf face in the app is disabled** — the 6.1j countdown is discharged. **Shipped: the FIRST plugin registry in the project** (`ui/common/lib/plugins/registry.ts` — **D-112's three axes `host`·`delivery`·`surface` in code for the first time**), with **`widgetRegistry` DERIVING from it** (*a widget is in the grid **because** it is a registered plugin with `surface: region`*), and a **read-only pane** (5th widget, `kind: system`, `surface: none`) in a shell-local modal. **Three honest rows, `[system]` badge, no faked verbs.** The grounding held: **there was no registry to enumerate, so the milestone created the first one** — it did not project one out of Rust (J-499's rule; the inverse of D-114's geometry). **⚠️ CORRECTION 1 — A COUNTDOWN NAMES *WHO* FLIPS A FACE, NEVER *WHEN*. THIS MILESTONE WAS BUILT TOO EARLY AND THE RECORD IS WHY.** 6.1j wrote *“no face is enabled before its command exists, **and no milestone closes leaving its own face disabled** — 6.1k flips diskette/load, 6.1l flips gear.”* **Those are TWO rules wearing one sentence.** The first is a real guard (**never enable a control that resolves to nothing**). The second is a **SCHEDULE**, smuggled in beside it unexamined — and it **pulled a milestone forward because a face was waiting, not because the product needed it.** Joe: *“I honestly thought we would grey the widget manager till we have a working grid.”* **He was right; the schedule was Chat's.** → ***A disabled face with a NAMED OWNER is honest indefinitely. A countdown names WHO discharges it — never WHEN, because that turns a guard into a deadline and lets the shelf drive the roadmap.*** *(Kept, because it is not nothing: the derive is the seam every future region drops into, and it is verified. > **🔒 THE DOCK ENGINE (RENDERER B) — PHASE-0 LOCKED 2026-07-12. `docs/xgen-dock-engine-phase0.md`. THE CHAPTER THE WHOLE M-RP7 ARC RESTS ON — READ IT IN FULL BEFORE PROPOSING ANYTHING IN THIS ARC.** Renderer A (`region-shell`, M-RP6.1f) renders a **const** tree into flex boxes and **nothing mutates it** — *it looks like a dock and is a picture of one*. Renderer B turns it into one: tile frame, fold, splitter resize, drag-to-rearrange. **It is a RENDERER UPGRADE, not a region rewrite** — both renderers read one D-103 descriptor. **🔑 §0 — JOE'S AUTONOMY GRANT: Chat holds full authority over every mechanical aspect of this arc — descriptor, mutation algebra, gesture semantics, persistence, verification, milestone split — EXCEPT THE GRAPHICAL APPEARANCE OF THE CREATED ELEMENTS, WHICH IS JOE'S.** And per **N-090 appearance is WIDE**: gaps, spacing, sizing, tracks and layout are skin-tunable, so **stripe height, grip size, seam thickness, band thickness, the folded form and the hole raster are all Joe's, not Chat's.** *The grant is over mechanics, not over the record — findings, deviations and gaps are surfaced as always (D-065).*  

> **🔒 D-116 — THE DOCK REARRANGES; IT NEVER JOINS. LOCKED (Joe, 2026-07-13).** **A TARGET TILE IS AN ADDRESS, NOT A CONTAINER.** In a space-filling tree there is no empty space to drop *into* — the tree's only vocabulary is **relative**: *above that one · below · left · right*. **The drag verb is not "put A into B"; it is "insert A here, and *here* is expressed as an EDGE of B".** The target receives nothing; it is a **lookup key**. → **NO centre drop-zone** (the centre of a tile is **inert**) · **NO tabs, ever produced** · **NO docked/undocked mode** (a mode was only ever needed to discriminate split-vs-tab; and *a region is docked iff it appears in the layout tree* — **a query, not a flag**; two sources of truth for "is this docked" is a D-067 drift surface) · **NO `M-RP-ROVING` prerequisite** (a tab strip would have been roving's **5th** independent impl; it is not built, so the extraction stays filed and stays out of this arc). **Fold IS the stacking mechanism and it is the better one:** four folded stripes in a column is a tab strip lying on its side — except **several can be open at once** and **every label is always visible**. ***Tabs is what you reach for when you don't have fold. We have fold.*** **The door stays SHUT, not LOCKED:** `types.ts` keeps typing `tabs`, `resolve.ts` keeps dropping it with a DEV warn — zero cost, zero schema change if it is ever wanted. **⚠️ If a future milestone reaches for tabs it RE-OPENS §3 EXPLICITLY — it does not arrive as a rider on a drag milestone.** **⚠️ AND D-116 IS NOT WEAKENED BY HOLES (J-514/J-515):** §2's *"there is no empty space to drop into"* is now only *mostly* true — but **D-116's ground is Joe's own constraint (*"never mixing or joining"*), NOT the geometry.** The rhetoric was corrected; the decision stands untouched.

A proof about one node is not a proof about the tree. Fifth in one family: N-091 · N-097 · N-099 · N-109 · this. **A verified claim is only ever as wide as the case that was actually run.**)* → **new milestone: M-RP7.1b.** **⚠️ D-117 WAS DRAFTED AND DELIBERATELY NOT LOCKED** — Joe superseded it before it reached `DECISIONS.md`, and his word on the replacement was ***"honestly i have to see it in practice."*** **→ no fold decision enters the record until it is built and looked at.** *A `D` locked for a design its author says he needs to see first is a prediction wearing a decision's clothes.* **MEASURED (Chat re-drove every leg, Rule 5):** client registry **67 — quiescent, EMPTY store, NO SELECTION** · **⚠️ N-108 EXTENDED — THE REGISTRY BREATHES WITH THE *SELECTION* STATE TOO: 67 (none) → 71 (selection) → 65 (selection + self folded). A baseline that does not state its SELECTION state is unreadable** → every future baseline states **store state AND selection state** · `cargo test` **1517/0/62 IDENTICAL** (*proves* no Rust) · `npm test` **43** · `vite build` **168** · sampler catalogue **328** · ratios `[1,2,7,2]` exact at **1242px and 1252px**. **⚠️ FILED, NOT FIXED — A LIBRARY-WIDE DEFECT: `mergeClasses` DOES NOT DEDUPE**, and `envelope` already stamps the type-class from `name` (N-023) → **any component that ALSO writes a literal `class="X"` renders `class="X X"`.** Five registered elements do it today: `region-shell` · `self-panel` · `inspector-panel` · `combobox` · `plugin-list`. **Clair fixed `region-tile` only — correctly, that was her scope.** The sweep (or making `mergeClasses` dedupe — a **`$common` base change**) is **ITS OWN MILESTONE, NEVER A RIDER.** **TOOLING, measured not folklore:** ⚠️ **the CDP bridge WRAPS getters** — `get(id)` returns `{type, state}`; read **`get(id).state.foo`** (reading `get(id).foo` returns **all nulls, which looks exactly like a broken build**) · tile classes are `region-tile-stripe`/`-move`/`-title`/`-fold`/`-body`/`-resize` (**not** `-strip`/`-grip`) · ⚠️ **an attribute selector with an unquoted `#` throws a bare `EVAL ERROR`** (quote it, or iterate `querySelectorAll('[data-debug-id]')`) · escaped double-quotes inside `-Expression` break the harness (use single quotes in the JS, or `String.fromCharCode(39)`) · `npm test` lives in **`ui/sampler`**, `vite build` in **`ui/client`** · ⚠️ **`cargo test` EXCEEDS THE MCP TIMEOUT — run it DETACHED and poll the PID in SEPARATE SHORT CALLS**; a long `Start-Sleep` kills the shell **and takes the detached run with it**, leaving a truncated log that read **1195/0/60 — plausible, complete-looking and WRONG**, betrayed only by the **missing final `test result:` line**. ***A killed detached run leaves a MEASUREMENT-SHAPED ARTIFACT.***  

> **✅ M-RP7.1b — THE FOLD AXIS BECOMES THE USER'S CHOICE; SPLITS SHRINK-WRAP; THE HOLE GETS A FLOOR. CLOSED (J-516).** Design locked J-515; shipped `0f25e50` + `14eb4d8` [Clair] + the appearance fix [Chat]. Phase-0 → **v1.4**. **⚠️ STILL NO FOLD `D` — Joe has now SEEN it run and has not asked for the lock. D-116 stands alone.** Runbook `tasks/M_RP7_1B_FOLD_AXIS.md` (COMPLETED). **Zero Rust · zero sampler · zero node**, verified by diffstat. **🔑 THE FINDING WAS A SCREENSHOT.** M-RP7.1's own arc table said its purpose was *"this is where Joe sees it and corrects the appearance"* — **he did, and it corrected more than the appearance.** He folded two regions, saw the empty band, and raised three things; **they turned out to be one thing.** **⚠️ The holes are NOT caused by the new design — they are IN THE SHIPPED BUILD** (N-111). **① THE FOLD AXIS IS THE USER'S CHOICE — TWO BUTTONS:** `[<]` folds left (collapses **width** → vertical strip) · `[v]` folds top (collapses **height** → horizontal stripe) · **folded ⇒ the unused button is DISABLED, the used one unfolds.** **Two axes, NOT four directions.** *(Cost, honestly: §4.1's elegant free property — "drag a folded tile col→row and it re-orients itself" — **DIES**. **The right trade: it was elegant for the TREE and surprising for the PERSON.** And it finishes a sentence §4.1 only half-wrote — it made fold **available** everywhere and left the **direction** an accident of the tree.)* **`collapsed` stops being a flag and becomes a DIRECTION (`'width' | 'height'`) — STORED, because it is user intent, and user intent is not derivable from anything.** The **along/across mode** stays **derived** (a fact about the tree). **② 🔒 A SPLIT SHRINK-WRAPS WHEN ALL ITS CHILDREN FOLD ACROSS IT (§4.4) — the part Joe did not ask for, and the part that actually solves his screenshot.** **Fold is a LEAF verb; a split's width is a SPLIT property living in the PARENT's `sizes[]` — a leaf verb cannot reach a split property**, so the 2/12 column stayed 2/12 wide no matter how its children folded. **❌ `collapsed` on a SPLIT node was REJECTED on Joe's own lock:** a leaf takes its title from `CLIENT_PLUGINS.name` and **a split has NO NAME** → name field + naming UI + **chrome on every split, nested, forever** = ***a group container promoted back to a MAIN FORM***, the exact thing §5 killed. **✅ Instead:** all children folded **across** ⇒ the split shrink-wraps, **the weight returns to its siblings by the `flex` they already have**, unfold one child ⇒ it re-inflates, **weights never mutated**. **Costs NOTHING NEW:** no descriptor field (**derived** — a stored flag goes stale the instant a child unfolds, **D-067 in miniature, twice in one arc**), no split chrome, and the mechanic is **byte-for-byte the folded leaf's** (`flex: 0 0 auto`). **⚠️ Mixed folds (`[<]`+`[v]`) deliberately DO NOT shrink-wrap** — column stays wide, hole opens, raster explains it. ***No magic, no guessing what they meant.*** **③ 🔒 HOLES ARE LEGAL, PAINTED, INERT (§4.5).** **§7.1's *"no holes, rectangles only"* is AMENDED by the man who accepted it 24 h earlier, in the open.** The hole is **flex leftover space inside a split, not an element** → **the raster is a BACKGROUND on the split container: zero new DOM, one skin rule**, shipped **provisional** (*you cannot tune a raster under holes you have not seen*). **🔒 AND THE LOCK FOR M-RP7.4: A HOLE IS NOT A DROP TARGET — D-116 says a target tile is an ADDRESS, and a hole has NO ADDRESS.** *(Drop on the EDGE of the tile above it. **Allow drops into holes and we have quietly built free 2-D placement and retired the tree.**)* **⚠️ D-116 IS NOT WEAKENED — its ground is Joe's constraint, not the geometry. Correct the rhetoric; do not touch the decision.** **⚠️ NO `D` LOCKED, ON JOE'S WORD: *"honestly i have to see it in practice."*** D-117 is dead without ever having been written, **and the replacement is NOT being locked either.** Phase-0 v1.3 carries the design — *that is what a Phase-0 is for*; `DECISIONS.md` carries **what Joe has decided, and he has told us he has not.** ***A `D` locked for a design its author says he needs to see first is a prediction wearing a decision's clothes.*** **🔑 TWO GROUNDED FINDS THAT CHANGED THE RUNBOOK BEFORE IT WAS WRITTEN:** **① `migrate` DOES NOT EXIST** — grepped: the word appears **only in comments** (`types.ts:16-17`, `layout-default.ts:63`); **`version` has been bumped TWICE and there has never been a migrate FUNCTION.** → **7.1b CREATES it.** *A path described in three documents and implemented in none is not a path; it is a plan.* **② ⚠️ A LAYOUT CAN ALREADY BE ON DISK, and the Phase-0 said the opposite BY ACCIDENT** — §12's *"nothing writes `session.layout`"* is **true**, and it was **read as "nothing writes a layout"**, which is **false**: **`app_client.svelte:227` has persisted layouts inside NAMED UI STATES since M-RP6.1k.** Measured on disk: `named: {}` → **migration is free TODAY — BY LUCK, NOT BY DESIGN; one diskette click ends that.** → **the answer is not "don't click the diskette", it is WRITE THE REAL MIGRATE** (`v2→v3`: each `collapsed: true` leaf takes its parent's `dir` → explicit direction — the old derived rule made honest, ~10 lines) **— DoD: EXERCISED in vitest against hand-built `v2` trees under BOTH parent kinds. Fed, not asserted (N-091, applied to the one branch in this codebase that has NEVER ONCE RUN).** **🔒 §4.1 AND §4.4 SHIP TOGETHER, NEVER APART — two buttons without the shrink-wrap is a thin strip beside a huge hole, STRICTLY WORSE than today.** ***Shipping the disease and the cure a week apart is how a bad appearance gets defended.***

READ THIS BEFORE THE NEXT LEG.** **① THE MIGRATE RAN — FOR THE FIRST TIME EVER.** Grounded before the runbook: **`migrate` DID NOT EXIST** — the word was **only in comments**; `version` had been bumped **twice** with **no function at all**. *A path described in three documents and implemented in none is not a path; the record stays `v2` on disk and re-migrates every load. **Idempotent, non-destructive, never silently edits a file the user wrote.** **② ⚠️ THE DEVIATION WAS CORRECT AND THE RUNBOOK WAS WRONG.** Clair flagged (Rule 6) `migrateLayout(raw, **fallback**)` instead of the runbook's bare `migrateLayout(raw)` → **`resolve.ts` is `core` and `DEFAULT_LAYOUT` is SHELL-LOCAL; B would have pointed `>`, at the content). **Only the HEIGHT button spoke disclosure** (`v` = *"my body is below"*) — straight from Joe's original `[<][V]` sketch, **which mixes the two conventions in one stripe** — **and the skin comment directly above the rotations already described convention A correctly.** ***The label was wrong, the prose was right, and one rotation value contradicted both.*** **🔒 THE RULE: THE CHEVRON ALWAYS POINTS WHERE THE REGION WILL GO IF YOU PRESS IT** — enabled or disabled, folded or not; the **disabled** button keeps its open glyph, still truthfully naming what it WOULD do. **The fix was TWO ROTATION VALUES SWAPPED.** → **N-116: re-reading the chapter would NEVER have caught this — the CHAPTER was self-consistent and the CODE was not.** **④ `--region-fold-gap: 0` is a KNOB, not a reservation — and ONLY because of `display: inline-flex`.** On the shipped `display: inline` a `gap` is **INERT**, and **an inert knob IS a reservation** (§4.3.1 — RESERVE NOTHING). **The test: can Joe change the value TODAY and see the render move? One diskette click and the client reads 71 FOREVER** — N-108's shape, except **the "data file" is one the USER writes with a button.** **⚠️ AND `71` IS NOW AMBIGUOUS: selection-active = 71; zero-selection + one-saved-state = 71. SAME NUMBER, TWO CAUSES.** **Axes: quiescence (N-105) · store (N-108) · selection (N-112) · named-state count (N-115).** **⚠️ One anomaly FILED, NOT EXPLAINED (Joe: *"leave it right now"*):** one launch rested at **71** with Joe's **real identity** auto-selected; three later fresh launches all rested at **67, no selection**. `self-panel` selects on `onActivate` (a click), never on mount — **something activated it and Chat could not establish what. What is in the hole right now is a **dev plate with no background defined**. A reactive backdrop is fine; a clickable one retires the tree.*** **⚠️ IT RETIRES §4.5's *"zero new DOM, one skin rule"*:** a CSS background does solid/gradient/pattern/keyframes, but **NOT a canvas, a shader, or anything reactive** — the dynamic plate **is an ELEMENT**. → **today's dot raster is the placeholder the plate REPLACES, not the seed it grows from** — but it is **PROMOTED, NOT DELETED**: it becomes the first **system** plate widget, so the socket ships **FED** (an unfed branch is an unverified branch, D-065/N-091). **⚠️ THE GATE IS A LOCK, NOT A PREFERENCE:** *"…sets it by its own **setting**"* — **there is no settings mechanism, deliberately.** J-513 filed the Ch6 `settings_schema`-vs-plugin-component collision as **explicitly undecided**, binding: ***nothing is built toward either until the grid works.*** **Joe: *"that is why i don't want to solve it now. now has priority widget grid with functional empty regions. background widget we can create after the grid concept works."*** **🔒 THIS ARC RESERVES NOTHING FOR IT** — no prop on `region-shell`, no descriptor key, no store, no manifest slot (*a key nothing writes is a key nobody has round-tripped* — the M-RP6.1k finding). **ZERO IMPACT ON M-RP7.2.**  

> **🎛️ THE GRID'S TWO SPACING KNOBS — NEW (Joe, 2026-07-14). Phase-0 §4.5.2.** Grounded: `.region-shell` had **NO padding** (and `.app-center` sets `padding: 0` explicitly, J-499's D5), and `.region-split` carried a **hardcoded `gap: 1px` — not even a knob**. Both are now **PROVISIONAL** tokens on `.region-shell`: **`--region-pad: 6px`** = the gap between the **GRID and the FRAME EDGE**, set **non-zero on purpose so it is visible for development inspection** · **`--region-seam: 1px`** = the gap **BETWEEN tiles**, same hairline, same value, now tunable. **🔑 `--region-seam` SURVIVES M-RP7.2:** the flex `gap` becomes a real seam **ELEMENT** (a `gap` cannot be hit-tested) and **this token becomes its thickness** — one token, both eras, so retuning it never means finding two places. **🔒 THE PERIMETER IS NOT A SEAM AND CAN NEVER BECOME ONE:** seams exist **only BETWEEN a split's children** (the `{#each}` interior) — **there is no leading or trailing seam** — so the outer edge can never grow a drag cursor or a hit zone. *It costs the splitter milestone nothing and it cannot lie to the user about a drag that does not exist.* **Ratios UNAFFECTED** — flex distributes over the reduced content box, so `[1,2,7,2]` still holds **exactly**; only the absolute px shrink by `2×pad`. Global `box-sizing: border-box` (`xgen-normalize.css:21`, grounded) means the padding cannot overflow the frame. **Registry unchanged. No component, no Rust.**  

> ### 🔑 **N-120 — THE ONE DEFECT M-RP7.2 SHIPPED. CLAIR FILED IT "LATENT, OUT OF SCOPE"; CHAT REACHED IT; IT INVERTS A RESIZE. → A REQUIRED LEG OF M-RP7.3, NOT A FILED ITEM.**
> **`region-node` threads `path` from the RESOLVED child index; `resizeSplit` walks the DESCRIPTOR by that path** — and **`resolve.ts` DROPS** (unknown leaves · `tabs` · all-dropped splits). **Any drop and the two index spaces diverge.** **REACHED, NOT ARGUED:** plant ONE unknown `widgetId` → descriptor has 3 children, **2 tiles paint, 1 seam** (between `spaces` and `rooms`) → **drag it RIGHT to ENLARGE `spaces`** → descriptor `[1,1,1]` → **`[1660,340,1000]`**: **`spaces` HALVED (660→335px) and `rooms` GREW.** ***The resize did the exact opposite of the gesture*** — the pair resized was `ghost ↔ spaces`. **And 55% of the row's weight now belongs to a widget that does not exist** — which **M-RP7.5 would write to DISK**, and which would **reappear eating half the screen** if that widget returned. **🔒 THE ASYMMETRY, PROVEN IN THE SAME POISONED LAYOUT: FOLD IS DROP-SAFE (it addresses by `regionId` — an IDENTITY); RESIZE IS DROP-FRAGILE (it addresses by `path` — a POSITION).** ***A position is only an address in the tree it was counted in.*** **🔑 THE RULE, BIGGER THAN THIS ARC: a DERIVED VIEW MAY RENUMBER — an index into a derived view is NOT an address into the source.** Either the resolved node carries its **source index**, or every mutation addresses by **identity**. *`resolve.ts` already knows the descriptor index at the moment it drops — it just throws it away.* **⚠️ Unreachable in today's build** (all 8 ids registered, `tabs` never produced — D-116) — **reachable the first time a widget id is RETIRED OR RENAMED and a user loads a saved workspace** (the W-13 reconcile case; M-RP7.1b proved the Load dialog is three clicks away). ***"Unreachable today" is the argument that has now been wrong FIVE times here*** (N-091 · N-097 · N-099 · N-109 · N-116). **→ M-RP7.3 OPENS WITH IT: addressing IS algebra, and a misaddressed `resize` nudges two integers while a misaddressed `move` RELOCATES A PANEL INTO THE WRONG BRANCH.**  

> **🧪 `cdp-debug.ps1` GAINS `-KeepSelection` (J-519) — because A HARNESS THAT CAN ONLY PASS IS A WEAK HARNESS.** The harness was clearing the selection before every gesture, so it **AVOIDED** N-118 instead of **TESTING** it. **CONTROL (clear suppressed): two drags on the tile body → `mousedown, mousemove, dragstart` — AND THEN NOTHING. No `mouseup`.** **N-118 reproduced ON THE WIRE, with the native drag finally VISIBLE** (at J-518 it was only *inferred*). **SUBJECT: the seam, same conditions, a live 21-char selection → both consecutive drags COMMITTED, no `dragstart`, `mousedown: false`** — because **`preventDefault()` on `pointerdown` SUPPRESSES the compat mouse events** and the pointer events carry the drag. **Measured, not assumed.**

Phase-0 §4.5.2.** **⚠️ J-517 shipped TWO tokens at TWO values** (`--region-seam: 1px`, `--region-pad: 6px`) — **Joe found it by looking at the screen** and drew two arrows: *the regions were glued to each other and floating off the frame.* ***A gap that means one thing at the edge and another between tiles is not a knob; it is a bug with two names.*** **Both now DERIVE from `--region-gap`** and must never drift apart again. **RE-DRIVEN: edge 5 · seam 5 · tile-to-tile 5 · grab zone 8px · registry 67 · MID-drag descriptor still `[1,2,7,2]`, AFTER `[198,102,700,200]` · behaviour-neutral.** **`--region-seam-hit` 3→1px** — it was sized for a 1px seam; against a 5px one it would reach into each neighbour's edge and eat clicks (§0, mechanical).  

> ### 🔑 **THE BACKDROP MOVED: `.region-split` → `.region-shell` (J-521). Joe: *"the not-transparent padding around whole grid is still there."***
> **He was right, and the width was never the problem — the SURFACE was. MEASURED:** the **split** painted `--s5` + the raster; **`.region-shell` AND `.app-center` were BOTH transparent.** → ***the gap BETWEEN regions showed the GRID's backdrop, and the gap AROUND the grid showed THE ROOM BEHIND THE GRID.*** **Two different surfaces for one conceptual gap.** *Joe's phrase — "the edge gap is made from outside" — described the implementation exactly, from the picture alone.* **🔒 NOW: the SHELL paints · the SPLITS are TRANSPARENT · the TILES are OPAQUE ⇒ every gap (seam · hole · perimeter) is the SAME ONE SURFACE.** **🔒 AND THIS IS ALREADY THE `M-RP-PLATE` ARCHITECTURE, arrived at early and for free** — that milestone now only swaps a `background-image` for a real element. **⚠️ `.region-split` MUST NEVER GET A BACKGROUND AGAIN** (it would re-split the surface *and* block the gap model). **⚠️ NEAR-MISS: a two-hunk `Filesystem:edit_file` failed on its SECOND hunk, so NEITHER applied — while a separate edit removing the old background DID.** For one build **nothing painted the grid at all.** ***edit_file is all-or-nothing PER CALL, not per hunk — and the CDP read caught it, not the diff. A diff that looks applied is not a render.***

ALL THREE WERE INTERNALLY CONSISTENT. NONE WAS CHECKED AGAINST THE THING IT CLAIMED ABOUT.** ***A dependency you have not TRIED TO BREAK is a dependency you have ASSUMED.*** *N-116: the record can be self-consistent while the code is not. N-118: my reasoning can be. This: my ARCHITECTURE can be — and a lock icon does not make it true.*  

> **⏸️ `M-RP-RESTART` — FILE ▸ RESTART **AND** REVERT. FILED (Joe, 2026-07-14; J-520). LANDS WITH M-RP7.5.** Joe asked for a File ▸ Restart item, *"disabled in states where it cannot be executed — no problem for me."* **The guard is right; grounding it split the item in TWO.** **🔑 `Restart`** (bounce the process) is **real today** — **`tauri-plugin-process` is ALREADY in `xgen-client/Cargo.toml`** and that plugin *is* `restart()`/`exit()`. **⚠️ BUT IT IS DECLARED AND NEVER WIRED** — no init in any `xgen-client/src/*.rs`, no frontend use: **a dependency nothing consumes.** **⚠️ `Revert`** (*"no automatic save on exit, load last automatic save"*) **— THERE IS NO AUTOMATIC SAVE: the grid does not write `session.layout` until M-RP7.5.** → **"restart without saving" and "restart" are the SAME THING today, and `Revert` would be greyed 100% of the time — not "in some moments".** ***A permanently-disabled item is not a guard; it is a promise*** — the painted-dead chrome this project keeps refusing (J-500 · 6.1j), and **the identical reason M-RP7.6's grid lock cannot ship early.** **→ ship both LIVE, together, with M-RP7.5** — the milestone that creates the state they discard. **⚠️ Named now: after M-RP6.3 (the send verb) a restart tears down a live WS that may hold UNSENT MESSAGES.** *Restart gets more expensive the more the app actually does.*  

> **🎨 THE REGION'S OWN EDGE — `--region-border: 1px` · `--region-edge: var(--s4)` (Joe, 2026-07-14; J-522). Joe: *"give each region 1px very fine smooth border. When gap is 0, there is no sense of regions, all is melted."* He tested the extremes (0 and 20px) and settled on `--region-gap: 4px`.** **⚠️ THE COLOUR COULD NOT BE `--s5` ANY MORE — J-521 MADE `--s5` THE BACKDROP**, so an `--s5` edge is **invisible at any gap > 0**. The edge must read against **both** neighbours: darker than the backdrop (`--s5 #343b47`), lighter than the tile body (`--s #16181c`). **`--s4 #2a2f38` does both.** *A token that was correct for a hairline stopped being correct the moment the surface behind it changed.* **🔑 N-121 — AND A REAL `border` BROKE THE ARC'S BASELINE. MEASURED FIRST: `[1,2,7,2]` came back `[1, 1.97, 6.90, 1.97]`.** With `flex: n 1 0` flex distributes the **FREE** space by weight, and then each item's **BORDER BOX = content + 2×border** — a **constant** added to every tile. **Content boxes stay proportional; border boxes do not — and EVERY rect JS can measure is a border box.** **⚠️ WORSE THAN COSMETIC: the splitter computes its drag fraction from the neighbours' `getBoundingClientRect()` widths, so a border would have biased the resize arithmetic by `2×border` per tile — systematically, silently, forever, and nothing would ever fail.** **✅ FIX: `outline` + `outline-offset: -1px`** — identical hairline, **ZERO layout impact**; **re-measured `[1,2,7,2]` EXACT**, `--region-min` still 22, folded strip still 22px with **zero overflow**, clamp still stops at 22 without folding. *(An inset `box-shadow` is also layout-free but paints BELOW the children — the stripe's `--s2` would cover the top hairline. An outline paints ABOVE. ⚠️ Tiles are not focusable today; if one ever needs a focus ring it must NOT be an outline on this element.)*  

> **🔑 N-122 — AND JOE'S OWN EXTREME TEST HAD A HOLE HE COULD NOT SEE.** He set `--region-gap: 0`, saw it render, and reported *"the system withstands it"*. **It renders — but the SEAM ELEMENT *IS* `--region-gap` WIDE, so at 0 the seam is ZERO PX**, and `--region-seam-hit` was a **constant 1px** → a **2px grab target: alive, and ungrabbable by a human.** *He tested that it did not break; he did not test that he could still drag it.* **✅ THE HIT AREA NOW COMPENSATES FOR THE GAP:** `max(1px, calc((8px - var(--region-gap)) / 2))` → **a CONSTANT grab zone at every setting. MEASURED (`elementFromPoint` swept at each value): 9px @ gap 4 · 9px @ gap 0 · 22px @ gap 20 — and THE DRAG COMMITS AT GAP 0** (MID `[1,2,7,2]` untouched, AFTER `[180,120,700,200]`). ***A knob that silently kills a gesture at one end of its range is not a knob.*** **⚠️ `calc()` IS SAFE HERE ONLY BECAUSE THIS TOKEN IS NEVER READ BY JS** — `getComputedStyle` does **not** resolve `calc()` in a custom property; it returns the raw token stream and `parseFloat` gives **`NaN`**. **`--region-min` and `--region-snap` ARE read by JS and must stay PLAIN VALUES.**  

> **⚠️ N-123 — AND THE INSTRUMENT THEN BROKE JOE'S TOKEN, AND HE REPORTED IT AS A BUG IN HIS OWN CSS.** Joe: *"something happens, `--region-gap: 4px` doesn't work anymore."* **It was not his CSS.** Proving the drag at gap 0 (N-122) needed the token set in **one** CDP call and the drag in the **next**, so the override could not be undone inside a single eval — it went on as an **inline style** on `.region-shell` **and was never removed.** **⚠️ VITE'S HMR HOT-PATCHES CSS WITHOUT A RELOAD, SO THE INLINE OVERRIDE SURVIVED EVERY EDIT HE MADE** — inline beats the stylesheet; his 4px was applied and then silently overridden, indefinitely, **by a leftover from a test he never ran.** **🔒 RULES (in `CDP_DEBUG_HARNESS.md`): a probe that persists a mutation across calls OWES A CLEANUP CALL, and the cleanup is PART OF THE PROBE · any session that touched inline styles ends with `location.reload()` · prefer reloading between token variants over inline overrides.** ***N-118 said the instrument can lie to ME. N-123 says it can lie to JOE — and he has no way to tell that the thing on his screen is not the thing in the file.***  

> ### 🔑 **AND THE THING IT WAS LOCKED TO FIX DOES NOT EXIST (N-124). ⚠️ "tile↔hole is 0" IS NOT OBSERVABLE.**
> The claim sat in the skin, in Phase-0 §4.5.2, in this PLAY block, and in the milestone's own runbook — **which Chat wrote before measuring it.** **A/B (inject the OLD geometry live, fold a tile ACROSS to build a real hole): folded-tile insets 4/4 vs 4/4 · distance-to-hole 774.8 vs 774.8 · the full gap census 4 EVERYWHERE under BOTH.** **🔒 Since J-521 moved the backdrop to `.region-shell`, A HOLE AND A GAP ARE THE SAME SURFACE** — a tile "butting onto a hole" is indistinguishable from a tile with a gap, *because there is nothing on the far side of that gap to be separated from.* **ZERO VISUAL DELTA.** **FOURTH TIME, SAME SHAPE:** N-118 my *reasoning* · J-521 my *architecture* · N-116 the *record* · **N-124 the *justification*** — and a 🔒 icon changed nothing except how confidently it got repeated.
>
> **🔒 JOE SHIPPED IT ANYWAY, ON THE RIGHT REASON: IT DELETES A MECHANISM, NOT A TOKEN.** The perimeter was the *shell's padding*, the inter-tile gap the *seam's thickness* — **two mechanisms, two tokens, which had already drifted apart once** (J-517: 1px vs 6px; Joe caught it by looking). **They can never drift again, because they no longer exist.** And **a gap that lives on the REGION travels with it when M-RP7.4 moves it**; a gap that lives on the GRID exists only where the grid puts a seam.

> ### ⚠️ **N-124a — TWO BASELINE NUMBERS IN THIS ARC WERE MORE PRECISE THAN THEIR MEASUREMENT. USE THE NEW ONES.**
> **`[1,2,7,2]` WAS NEVER BIT-EXACT.** Recorded "EXACT" at four window widths; at full float precision it is **`[1, 2.000112, 7.000224, 2.000112]` — and the OLD geometry returns `2.000114`.** ~0.004px of **pre-existing Chromium sub-pixel, in BOTH models** — **which is exactly what proves the margin adds no bias** (N-121's border broke it by **1.5%**; this is **0.006%**). **And the clamp stops at `22.29px`, not "exactly 22"** (integer-weight rounding, L2; the OLD geometry rounds to **21.89 — just BELOW the floor**). ***A number you rounded before you recorded it is a number you can no longer use as a control.***
>
> **MEASURED (Chat re-drove every leg, Rule 5):** perimeter **4/4/4/4** · **14 adjacent tile pairs all exactly 4**, incl. pairs crossing a split boundary (**it composes at depth**) · **MID-drag** descriptor `[1,2,7,2]` untouched with the button still down, **AFTER** `[194,106,700,200]` (pair total 300 invariant, untouched siblings ×100) · grab zone **9px @ gap 0 · 9px @ gap 4 · 20px @ gap 20**, seam wins at x=113–115 *inside* the neighbour's border box (**N-119's `z-index` re-proven**) · registry **67** (the zero-width seam registers nothing) · `npm test` **59** · `vite build` **169** · zero Rust **by scope**.

> ### 🔒 **THE NEXT FIVE, SEQUENCED (Joe-locked, J-564). The ordering argument is about WHAT MAKES LATER WORK SMALLER, not about size.** **① 🟢 `M-RP-MSG-NEWLINE` — preserve typed line breaks in the rendered message** · **② `M-RP-PROCESSOR-SEED`** · **③ `core`'s prop-less `Record<string, Component>`** · **④ `M-RP-COMPOSER-SCROLL`** · **⑤ `M-RP-PROCESSOR-RENDER`.** **🔑 WHY ① IS FIRST, AND IT IS NOT SIZE: solving line breaks in CSS TAKES `<br>` OFF KIND-4's ALLOWLIST** — *every element removed from a sanitiser's allowlist is one less thing that has to be right forever.* **🔑 WHY ③ PRECEDES ⑤: two of its six sites are `message.svelte` and `message-stream.svelte`, the exact files ⑤ will open** — fix the types first and the message family is touched **once with correct types instead of twice.** **⑤ IS LAST DELIBERATELY** — by then `<br>` is handled, the family is typed and the scroll is right, **so it can be ONLY about the sanitiser.** *Chat named ①-vs-② as genuinely interchangeable; the only orderings carrying a real argument are newline-before-RENDER and typing-before-RENDER.* [⚠️ ITEM ① STALE 2026-07-29 (J-615) — M-RP-MSG-NEWLINE is CLOSED at L183 of this same head (J-565); tasks/M_RP_MSG_NEWLINE.md reads COMPLETED v1.0. The rest of the sequencing lock stands. Block stays live.]

> ### 🟡 **M-RP-PROCESSOR-RENDER — THE RENDER-SIDE ENGINE AND KIND-4 MARKUP. 🟡 PENDING, FIFTH (filed J-564).** **🔑 JOE ASKED WHETHER ALL PROCESSOR MODES WERE TESTED, ESPECIALLY RICH TEXT / `.md`. THE ANSWER IS NO, BECAUSE HALF THE ARCHITECTURE HAS NEVER BEEN BUILT.** Grounded against D-099 (*two engines × four rule-kinds*): **edit-side engine BUILT** (`processor.ts`) · **kind 1 transformer BUILT, wired at exactly one call site** · **kinds 2 (converter) and 3 (filter/guard) built in `$common` but with ZERO CLIENT CALL SITES — sampler only** · **render-side engine (`use:render`) DOES NOT EXIST** · **kind 4 `string → safeHTML` DOES NOT EXIST.** D-099 says it outright: *the markup/sanitiser sink is not built this arc* (D-065, no empty machinery). ***There was nothing to test — markdown rendering has never been built.*** **THE DESIGN IS ALREADY WRITTEN DOWN:** `paragraph.svelte:13–19` — WordStar/markdown-lineage `_x_`/`*x*`, whitelist `<strong>`/`<em>`/`<br>`, an escape character, links named as *"the one risky add"*, landing as a `$common` `use:render` action. **🔒 LOCKED BEFORE IT IS BUILT: `<br>` COMES OFF THE ALLOWLIST, because M-RP-MSG-NEWLINE solves line breaks in CSS.** The two routes are **not** equivalent — `white-space: pre-wrap` keeps the body a **TEXT NODE with no `{@html}`, no sanitiser and NO XSS SURFACE**, while kind-4 `<br>` requires **opening `{@html}` and trusting a sanitiser on every message body, including ones that arrived over the wire from another identity.** *Recorded now precisely so nobody implements `<br>` as the newline answer and opens the sanitiser surface for something CSS already solved — N-032's anti-`{@html}` lean and `message.svelte:40`'s "never `{@html}`" were protecting exactly this.* **⚠️ THIS IS THE FIRST THING IN THE PROJECT THAT WOULD RENDER ANOTHER IDENTITY'S TEXT AS MARKUP, ON A NETWORK WITH NO ANONYMITY. A SECURITY LOCK, JOE'S, AND THE ONE MILESTONE HERE THAT MUST NOT BE SCOPED IN A SINGLE SITTING** — placing it fifth buys that time honestly rather than by stalling. **A D-number for the sanitiser is Joe's to mint when it locks.**

> ### 🟡 **THE DESIGN WALK BEHIND IT — SIX MEASURED FINDINGS AND TWO JOE CORRECTIONS (J-598).** ① **THE OUTAGE IS BIDIRECTIONAL** — a send is queued as **intent, not an event** (`resident.rs:705-727`; the drain builds/signs/anchors at write time), so during an outage **the user's own message never becomes an event at all**; no persistent outbox; `SendOutcome` stays honest. ② **THE CATCH-UP MECHANISM IS BUILT, TESTED AND NEVER CALLED BY THE GUI** — `SyncRequest`→`HistoryBatch`+`SyncComplete` (`app.rs:1752-1784`), `collect_sync_history` (`fanout.rs:478`) member-only/paginated/cursor-resumable, five passing tests, used by `ai_service.rs` + `batch.rs`; **`resident.rs` never issues one**, and no sync cursor is persisted. ③ 🔑 **THE CLIENT HAS NO MESSAGE HISTORY AT ALL** — `ingest` is in-memory, capped `INGEST_CAP = 500`, with a `dropped` counter: **a session window, not a cache**; the startup block does **no history fetch of any kind** ⇒ **every start is a cold start with an empty conversation**, and the blackout gap is a special case of a permanent one. ④ 🔒 **SYSTEM ENTRIES ARE THREAD ENTRIES LOCAL TO ONE USER (Joe, WhatsApp *delete-just-for-user*)** ⇒ **the rendered thread is a PER-USER PROJECTION of the shared record, not the shared record**; four consequences agreed — the thread becomes a **merge**, ordering meets two clocks, persistence is session-scoped **but COUPLED to message persistence** (or a reopened thread shows a gap with its explanation missing), and **additive and subtractive are ONE facility** so delete-for-me lands in the same local layer. **Drafted `D-130`; number and wording Joe's.** ⑤ 🔒 **NO MESSAGE CACHE (Joe): *"the client is just reader-sender. doesnt hold any users data."*** 🔑 **The lock sits a LEVEL ABOVE the question, so the D-093 crypto-shred tension never has to be adjudicated — the copy that would create it never exists.** ⚠️ **One measured exception: the address book DOES persist third-party data**, bounded by `T1_DEFAULT_RETENTION_DAYS = 182`; ⚠️ **whether eviction is WIRED or merely DECLARED is UNMEASURED — Chat's.** ⑥ 🔒 **THE OUTBOX SURVIVES THAT LOCK** — Joe's *"purge … not whole enblock"* maps onto the four-way `SendOutcome`: **purge on `accepted`, keep on everything else**; per-item is load-bearing because a block purge discards a `timed_out` beside a confirmed one. 🔑 **An outbox item BECOMES a cache the instant the node accepts it — which is exactly when the rule drops it.** 🔓 Open: post-reconnect anchor timestamp · `timed_out` auto-retry vs manual · the sibling's name. → J-598.

> ### 🔑 **H1 / H2 — THE ADDRESS BOOK AT REST, AND THE ONE PLACE THE THESIS ACTUALLY BITES (J-598).** ⚠️ **THERE IS NO IDENTITY LOOKUP VERB ON THE WIRE — NONE.** Records are **derived from a Space DAG drain** (`fill_and_members`, `desktop.rs:644-697`), independently confirming `M_RP_ADDRESS_BOOK.md` §5. ⇒ Joe's *send pubkeys, get visit cards* is **new protocol** (wire `xgen-core/src/wire/types.rs:45` · node handler · **and the SPEC CHAPTER — in a protocol project the spec IS the deliverable**). **H1 — drop the persistence, keep the book in memory — needs NO protocol at all** and is nearly free: `loadMembers` fires the fill **unconditionally on every Space change**, so the file only buys the pre-first-fill window and restarts. 🔒 **Joe relaxed the resource lens** (*"i dont mind if we will need to add some cli … we will test it"*) — build cost is **not** the objection. 🔑 **THE THESIS ANSWER, ASKED IN THOSE TERMS: THE VERB IS THESIS-NEUTRAL; THE SCOPE RULE IS THESIS-BEARING.** No-anonymity means *within a Space you know who you are talking to*, **NOT** *any identity is globally queryable by anyone holding a pubkey* — **an unscoped verb converts the first into the second: a thesis change made by accident, at the wire level.** Member-scoped, as `collect_sync_history` already is. ⚠️ **Recorded as Chat's READING from the project record — the Ch0–Ch2 read is OWED before any of it reaches a spec.** 🔓 H1 and H2 are Joe's and belong to neither current milestone. → J-598.

> ### 🛑 **M-INTRO-POLICY — receiver-side render policy: PHASE-0 LANDED (J-739, 2026-08-16). ITS STATED INPUT IS NOT ON THE WIRE, AND A DESIGN WALK GAVE THE MILESTONE A SECOND PLANE. 🟡 PENDING — ELEVEN OPEN QUESTIONS, ALL JOE'S. NO PRODUCT CODE.** `tasks/M_INTRO_POLICY_PHASE0.md` **v1.1 ACTIVE**.

> 🛑 **HEADLINE — THE RECORD PRICED THE WRONG OBSTACLE.** The node reasons *"Tiers 2–4 do not exist ⇒ a Tier-2-plus gate excludes everyone."* True, and **the weaker of two.** **`identity.record` — the ONLY identity lookup response the protocol has — carries EIGHT fields and NO `trust_assertion`, no tier, no `revoked`, no `update_version` (`xgen-core/src/wire/types.rs:453-471`), while the stored `IdentityRecord` carries TWELVE (`registry.rs:32`).** ⇒ **THE WIRE PROJECTION IS NARROWER THAN THE STORE AND THE TIER IS ONE OF THE FOUR FIELDS DROPPED** — so **even if Tier 2 shipped tomorrow a receiver's client could not read a sender's tier.** 🔑 **AND `auth_tier` IS THE WRONG OBJECT ANYWAY:** `verify_tier_assertion(assertion_tier, space_auth_tier)` is a **Space JOIN-GATE FLOOR** checked at admission (`tiers.rs:158-168`), not a property of a sender ⇒ **in a DM it is a constant `1` by construction and says nothing about who is speaking.** 🛑 **THE UNFED BRANCH ALREADY SHIPPED ON EXACTLY THIS INPUT:** `address_book.rs` has `trust_assertion: Option<Value>` + a reader `trust_lapsed()` + a guard test asserting *"an ordinary fetched record has no assertion"* — **a field, a reader, a test and no feeder, `N-091` at protocol scale.**

> 🔑 **THE NAMED PRECEDENT IS INERT AND ITS OWN MODULE DOC SAYS SO IN CAPITALS.** `NodePolicy` — 47 hits, 5 files, **all node-side**, zero in `xgen-client/`, zero in `ui/`; `node_policy.rs:26-32`: ***"FORK X (NP-D3): the store is INERT this arc — nothing in the running Node reads it"***, the two admin verbs its **sole consumer** ⇒ **there is NO enforcement path in this codebase to copy.** It also mismatches on **key** (`HashMap<SpaceXgid, _>`, per hosted Space vs per receiver identity) and **principal** (node-operator authority #1 vs a user preference). ✅ What transfers: the **`absent == disabled`** prime invariant — `N-182` in a persisted store. 📌 **No `policy.*` namespace exists on the wire at all** — 61 renamed variants, nine namespaces, zero policy ⇒ a client-facing policy read is a **new namespace**.

> 🔑 **THEN JOE WALKED IT AND THE WALK MOVED MORE THAN THE AUDIT DID — FIVE OF ITS STEPS CORRECTED CHAT INSIDE THE SAME SESSION.** 🔒 **The DM-privacy argument's strongest form is TIER-INDEPENDENT and is what got recorded: *third-party moderation exists to protect people who did not choose to be in the room, and a DM has no bystanders*** — either party's exit ends it at zero cost, where in a group leaving costs you the group. 🛑 **BUT THE SPEC DOES NOT SAY IT:** §3.16.1's five constraints have *"Private"* meaning ***not discoverable via Bootstrap Node directory*** — **discovery privacy, not content privacy** ⇒ **the autonomy is a SIXTH constraint that does not exist: a SPEC AMENDMENT, not a conformance fix.** ⚠️ **The collision is live** — §3.7.13.6 has automated moderation issue signed `membership.kick`/`membership.mute` **signed by the node operator's identity**, **nothing in §3.7.13 scopes itself away from DMs**, and **DM state already carries `member_temperature_visibility` at the `moderator` default** (`state.rs:465`, `:581`). 🔑 **The spec already draws the right boundary one section over:** §3.7.13.4's *"the home Node enforces visibility… the client does not implement filtering"* is **the exact inverse of `I1`** and **both are right** — temperature is `meta_atts` readable on ciphertext, content is not ⇒ ***the boundary is what the node can still enforce after PG-05.*** 🛑 **AUTONOMY IS A CARVE-OUT TO BUILD, NOT A PROPERTY TO PRESERVE:** `is_dm` has **zero hits in `xgen-node/`** ⇒ **every general-Space node setting already applies to DMs today, silently.** ⚠️ **Chat's own framing corrected at the site: that zero must NOT be read as "the constraints are not enforced"** — the five are enforced, in `xgen-core`'s state machine, which the node runs.

> 🔑 **THE MILESTONE GAINED A PLANE IT DID NOT HAVE, AND §9 PREDICTED IT WITHIN THE DAY** (*"a fifth option is the most likely defect in this file"*). **Gate the DM's CREATION, not the message's RENDERING.** 🔑 **AN ACCEPTANCE GATE DOES NOT INHERIT `D-143`'s EXPIRY DATE:** `I1` moves the filter client-side because after PG-05 the node cannot read **content**; an acceptance gate reads the **initiator's identity tier from the registry**, which the retention path proves the node already does. ***Identity is not encrypted; content is.*** ⇒ **node-side, permanent, ENFORCEABLE rather than advisory, and it CLOSES Q4 outright.** ✅ **The hook exists:** DM creation seeds a **`PendingInvite`** (`state.rs:416-430`) ⇒ **the recipient is PENDING, not a member, until `membership.join`.** 🛑 **THE ONE MEASUREMENT NOT TAKEN AND IT GATES THE PLANE: DOES THE CLIENT AUTO-JOIN A DM INVITE? If it does, a consent gate exists in the protocol and is being spent silently.** ✅ **The requirement has an additive home and works at T1:** `ModulePolicy.extra: BTreeMap<String, Value>` ⇒ a `dm_invite_required` key with **zero struct change**, the `xgen.intro.v1` shape. 🔑 **Discovery collapses Q6: the client needs ONE BOOLEAN, not `trust_assertion` wholesale** — no legal name, clearance or jurisdiction, **no regulated data.** 🛑 **CORRECTION: `space.join_request` is NOT the precedent it looks like** — `{ space_id, node_id }`, **node↔node federation**; **there is NO user-level request-to-join verb on the wire**, so a DM invite request is genuinely new protocol.

ruled out. **Letting the header WRAP NORMALLY and clamping it to ONE LINE** (`overflow-wrap: anywhere` · `max-height: 1lh` · `overflow: hidden`) makes the clip land **BETWEEN LINES, NOT BETWEEN LETTERS** ⇒ the rule holds **BY CONSTRUCTION**, zero JS, zero `text-overflow`, zero `line-clamp` (which would force an ellipsis back in). ✅ `overflow-wrap: anywhere` already has **3 precedents in the construction* (`N-032`) — and that precedent covers a **compile-time constant, which a display name is not.** **🛑 THE BUTTON CENSUS — THREE OF FOUR SKETCHED CONTROLS DO NOT SHIP:** `{button-remove-friend}` → **`friend` = ZERO hits** (the one match is `conn.rs:13` *"log-friendly"*) — **no friend model exists** · `{button-block}` → **240 `block` hits, EVERY ONE DAG/async plumbing** — no verb, no blocklist, no wire event · `{mutual-friends}` → **18 `mutual` hits, all concurrency prose**, and **`KnownSpace` carries NO member list** ⇒ **not even client-derivable** · `{button-wave}` → a wave is a **MESSAGE**, real today, **but not this leg** (Joe: *"later elements"*). **🔑 SHIPPING THEM GREYED WOULD REPLACE ONE DEAD CONTROL WITH THREE — INSIDE THE MILESTONE WHOSE PURPOSE IS TO DELETE ONE.** `D-113`'s correction governs: **a control is disabled only for a reason TRUE OF THAT SUBJECT and legible to the user**, and *"we never built blocking"* is not that reason. **FILED, NOT BUILT: `M-RP-BLOCK`.** **🔑 JOE'S QUESTION FOUND A REAL STATE AND THE PROTOCOL BACKS IT:** `IdentityRecord.display_name` is **`Option<String>`** (`registry.rs:34`); validation sits **inside `if let Some(name)`** so **absent is ACCEPTED**, while **empty string is REJECTED** (`DisplayNameInvalid`, 3009) with >128 and control chars (`registration.rs:514-520`); **`no_display_name_accepted`** asserts `is_none()` (`:1023`) beside **`empty_display_name_rejected`** (`:1005`). ⇒ **THE PROTOCOL DRAWS A TWO-STATE THE UI MUST RESPECT: *no name* IS LEGAL, *empty name* IS NOT** — a blank label can never arrive, so **`tail8` is sufficient and nothing more elaborate is needed.** **TWO live nameless cases reach the page and — answering Joe's own hypothesis — NEITHER IS AN OUTAGE:** ① **live-joined** (`MemberEntry.unresolved`, stamped by `addMember` when the roster gained a member via a membership delta and the book was never consulted — **transient**, > ### 🔓 **THE DM-ENTRY DESIGN CONVERSATION, BANKED — FOUR MILESTONES FILED AND `J-692` STILL UNRULED (J-701, 2026-08-08). ✅ SUPERSEDED IN PART BY J-702 ABOVE — `J-692` IS RULED. 🟢 `M-RP-MEMBER-ACT` PLAY.** No code. `M-RP-INTRO` · `M-INTRO-POLICY` · `M-RP-MEDIA` · `M-STREAM-LIVE` all filed 🟡 PENDING · `N-172` + `N-173` · notes **v1.14** · ROADMAP **v6.89**. **No new `D`.** 🛑 **NOTHING HERE IS LOCKED AND `J-692` IS STILL OWED BY JOE** — the arc closed by banking the design, deliberately, because four turns of good architecture had accumulated while a **dead control stayed shipped in the product.**
>
> 🔑 **WHAT JOE ACTUALLY SAID, AND WHAT HE DID NOT (`D-141`).** He **refused** the RMC context menu as the primary route — *"this has to be the most straight path"* — and said he is **not afraid of option (b)**, the Discord-style draft DM opened by LMC. ⚠️ **That is a direction, not a lock; `J-692` has not been uttered as a ruling and is recorded as OPEN.** He also **corrected his own proposal mid-conversation** — having asked whether the intro could be defined Space-side, he reached the conclusion the measurement supported: *"i realize that in dm is a special space, so it cannot be in some other space definition."*
>
> 🔑 **THE ARCHITECTURE WAS ALREADY THERE, TWICE, AND GROUNDING FOUND IT BEFORE CHAT ENDORSED ANYTHING.** Joe proposed *a special system canvas without user input* for a Discord-like entry draft. ✅ **It exists and is LOCKED:** `message-stream`'s `background?: WidgetMount[]` (§9.4, J-481) — a persistent fixed layer behind the log, chat-wallpaper style, **whose empty-state rule is already written**: *`background` unset **and** `count===0` → default paragraph. Never bare.* And *more than one type of message* is **already four on the wire** (`message.text` / `message.file` / `message.reaction` / `message.redact`), with `message.file` carrying MIME-typed `attachments` ⇒ **audio and video need no new kind and no new event type.** ***This is the J-699 failure mode NOT repeated: the file was read before the question was answered.***
>
> 🔑 **`N-172` — THE PROPERTY THAT DECIDES EVERY ONE OF THESE FEATURES, AND IT IS EASY TO SPEND BY ACCIDENT.** `WidgetMount` has **zero hits in any `.rs` file**: nothing on the wire carries one, so **every mount is named by the RECEIVING client**, from a widget it already has. **The sender contributes DATA; the receiver decides RENDERING.** One wire field would reverse that and make `props: Record<string, unknown>` attacker-controlled — `W-13`'s unknown-id drop protects you from a widget you do **not** have and from **nothing** inside one you do. ⚠️ **And the arc has not yet crossed a line:** `grid-plate` and `send-status` both project the reader's **own** state; a welcome intro is the first tenant rendering **someone else's**. ⇒ ***A canvas is a PROJECTION OF EVENTS, NEVER A CARRIER OF THEM.***
>
> 🛑 **THE SURFACE ARGUMENT, WHICH IS THE REAL CONTENT OF THE CONVERSATION.** An intro rendered as **system chrome** is stranger-authored content **in the system's voice, on first contact, before the recipient has agreed to anything** — `D-113` S-5's no-trust-chrome rule, and the classic unsolicited-first-contact vector. ⚠️ **Privacy does not mitigate it: the threat IS one sender and one target in private**, with no third party to notice and no community to report it. ✅ **Joe's own wording carried the fix** — *"he will **send** his nice draft"* ⇒ **it is a MESSAGE**: attributed, in the DAG, redactable, blockable, reportable, and symmetric for free.
>
> 🔑 **AND JOE'S ANSWER WAS BETTER THAN CHAT'S OBJECTION: FILTER ON THE RECEIVER, NOT THE SENDER.** The sender sends freely; the recipient's home node decides what renders — the email model, fail-safe by default. 🛑 **But it must be enforced in the CLIENT, and that is not a preference:** today a node can read DM content, **after PG-05 it holds ciphertext and cannot inspect, strip or rewrite anything** ⇒ a node-side filter ships with a known expiry date (`D-143`). ⚠️ **The auth-tier gate is right and cannot fire yet:** Tiers 2–4 *"are built in institutional collaboration with qualified organisations"* and do not exist, `build_dm_space_create_event` hardcodes `auth_tier: 1`, and **`auth_tier` has ZERO hits in `ui/`** ⇒ a Tier-2+ gate today excludes everyone, including Joe. **It re-opens honestly under `D-146` when the tier actually reaches the client** — a cost expiring, not taste changing.
>
> 📌 **`N-173` — A LATENT DESIGNATION COLLISION, CAUGHT BEFORE IT WENT LIVE (`D-134`).** *"Tier-1 / Tier-2"* already means **two unrelated things**: `AuthTier` (identity verification, Rust) and the processor **provenance** axis (code-vs-user trust, `transform.ts`). Every `tier` hit in `ui/` is the second; `auth_tier` appears in neither. **The collision is latent precisely because auth tiers have never reached the client** — which is why now is the moment. The processor axis is UI-local with no wire or spec footprint, so it is the cheaper side to rename. **The call and the words are Joe's.**
>
> 📌 **ONE PROPOSAL OF CHAT'S WAS RAISED AND WITHDRAWN IN THE SAME ARC:** a standalone `M-RP-CANVAS` to formalise the input-free surface. **Withdrawn once the socket was found to have two live tenants already** (`grid-plate`, `send-status`) — building a frame around a shipped thing, and a surface with no tenant is the reserved-unfed shape `D-065` warns about. **Recorded, not erased.** → J-701 · ROADMAP v6.89.

> ### ✅ **`M-RP-MEMBER-ACT` LEG C — R7 ACTS: THE ROW OPENS THE DM — CLOSED (J-700, 2026-08-08). THREE COMMITS: `524d4f7` · `b5d0908` · `6a6c066`. 🟢 `M-RP-MEMBER-ACT` STAYS PLAY — THE RMC CONTEXT MENU AND LEG C-bis REMAIN.** Runbook v1.1 → **v1.2 COMPLETED** · Leg C Phase-0 → **v1.5 COMPLETED** · **NEW `tasks/M_RP_MEMBER_ACT_LEG_C_BIS.md` v1.0 PENDING** · notes **v1.13** (`N-169`, `N-170`, `N-171`) · ROADMAP **v6.88**. **Click a member and their DM opens; the person lands on the bus so R8 shows the card.** Clair implemented all three legs from the locked runbook; Chat re-drove every gate independently and **the two agreed on every number.**
